{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014v58G9RDU6Ama8bQjgW3Cr","type":"tool_result","content":"# Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n2926558 feat(session): list a user's conversations from the store (#146)\na99a64b Merge pull request #141 from re-cinq/test/pin-open-criteria\n55bf05a Merge remote-tracking branch 'origin/main' into test/pin-open-criteria\nAGENTS.md\nCHANGELOG.md\nCLAUDE.md\nCODE_OF_CONDUCT.md\nCONTRIBUTING.md\nLICENSE\nREADME.md\nSECURITY.md\nadrs\ndocs\neslint.config.backlog.mjs\neslint.config.mjs\nexample\njest.config.js\nlayers.yaml\nnode_modules\npackage-lock.json\npackage.json\nscripts\nsmoke\nspecs\nsrc\ntsconfig.build.json\ntsconfig.example.json\ntsconfig.json\n# Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n  name: HAL Engine Jest suite\n  cwd: .\n  description: >-\n    The ts-jest unit suite over src/ and scripts/ (jest.config.js roots), run\n    on every pull request in .github/workflows/ci.yml as the \"Test\" step.\n    Selectors are \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by the\n    list command. Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite. Neither command builds first:\n    no test here reads dist/.\n  list: node scripts/lore-list-tests.mjs\n  run: node scripts/lore-run-test.mjs \"{selector}\"\n  coverage_format: lcov\ntest-commands.yml","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c9f653e3-de50-40d7-9dab-0757a826ee31","uuid":"7e3e4bcc-684d-4bb9-ab67-f8c3fa38e96e","timestamp":"2026-11-06T20:44:46.493Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n2926558 feat(session): list a user's conversations from the store (#146)\na99a64b Merge pull request #141 from re-cinq/test/pin-open-criteria\n55bf05a Merge remote-tracking branch 'origin/main' into test/pin-open-criteria\nAGENTS.md\nCHANGELOG.md\nCLAUDE.md\nCODE_OF_CONDUCT.md\nCONTRIBUTING.md\nLICENSE\nREADME.md\nSECURITY.md\nadrs\ndocs\neslint.config.backlog.mjs\neslint.config.mjs\nexample\njest.config.js\nlayers.yaml\nnode_modules\npackage-lock.json\npackage.json\nscripts\nsmoke\nspecs\nsrc\ntsconfig.build.json\ntsconfig.example.json\ntsconfig.json\n# Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n  name: HAL Engine Jest suite\n  cwd: .\n  description: >-\n    The ts-jest unit suite over src/ and scripts/ (jest.config.js roots), run\n    on every pull request in .github/workflows/ci.yml as the \"Test\" step.\n    Selectors are \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by the\n    list command. Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite. Neither command builds first:\n    no test here reads dist/.\n  list: node scripts/lore-list-tests.mjs\n  run: node scripts/lore-run-test.mjs \"{selector}\"\n  coverage_format: lcov\ntest-commands.yml","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_014v58G9RDU6Ama8bQjgW3Cr","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NGcwTA8xhXknix67qKb4UH","type":"tool_result","content":"# Conversation List\n\n| Field  | Value                 |\n| ------ | --------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#143 |\n| Status | Shipped               |\n\nResume gets a client back into one conversation: the id it names, or the user's most recently active one. Neither tells it which conversations exist, and every other member of `SessionStore` answers about a session the caller can already name, so a consumer wanting to offer a conversation list had to query the collection behind the engine's back, against a document shape and a key the engine owns. `listFor` answers the list instead, as summaries rather than sessions. **GDPR.** A summary is personal data — that a user held a conversation, and when — so it carries no conversation content, it is filtered to one user by the query rather than in application code afterwards, and the retention bound stays the consumer's, through the store's `maxAgeMs` and erasure methods and re-cinq/HALEngine#41.\n\n## The summary\n\n- A summary is `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount`, and nothing else: neither the conversation's entries nor its credentials, so a list can never hand over either, even for a session created with credentials ([validated by: carries only its four fields, so a list can never hand over a conversation or a credential](../../src/infrastructure/stores/sessionStoreListSignature.test.ts#L22)).\n- `listFor` is optional, like `latestFor` and `lookup` before it: a store written against the five original members still satisfies the interface, and both shipped stores implement it ([validated by: leaves a store with only the five original members satisfying the interface beside both shipped ones](../../src/infrastructure/stores/sessionStoreListSignature.test.ts#L11)).\n\n## The order a list answers in\n\n- Summaries come back most recent activity first ([validated by: answers newest activity first, headed by the very session latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L27)).\n- The first summary is the session `latestFor` returns, because both read the same activity ([validated by: answers newest activity first, headed by the very session latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L27)).\n- The order is total rather than merely sorted: two conversations last active in the same millisecond are ordered by session id, the larger first, which is what lets a page resume without dropping one of them ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on activity rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L141)).\n- In `InMemorySessionStore` that tie-break is the list's alone: `latestFor` breaks the same tie by creation order, so for two tied conversations the head of the list and the session a resume rejoins can differ ([validated by: breaks a tie on activity by session id, where latestFor takes the session created later](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L42)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` answers in order of `updatedAt`, which each `save` moves forward, and its first summary is the session its own `latestFor` returns ([validated by: answers the user's conversations newest saved first, headed by the one latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L31)).\n- It breaks a tie by `_id` in both members, so there the head of the list is the session a resume rejoins even when every conversation shares an `updatedAt` ([validated by: heads the list with the session latestFor returns even when every conversation shares an updatedAt](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L161)).\n\n## What a summary reports\n\n- `InMemorySessionStore` reports the moment `create` was called, the newest entry time as the activity, and the conversation's entry count ([validated by: reports a creation time, the newest activity and the entry count of each conversation](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L53)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` reports the document's `createdAt`, the `updatedAt` its last save wrote, and the counter written beside the entries ([validated by: reports the creation time, the last save and the entry count of each conversation](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L49)).\n- That counter is what a save writes, so a list never reads a conversation to count one ([validated by: counts a conversation from the field a save writes beside the entries](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L95)).\n- A document written before the counter existed reads as a conversation of no entries rather than failing the list, which is what the backlog documented in `docs/session-stores.md` is for ([validated by: reads a conversation saved before the counter existed as one of no entries, rather than failing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L79)).\n\n## Whose conversations\n\n- A list holds only the asking user's conversations: another user's never appear, and a user with none gets an empty list ([validated by: omits a conversation that aged out and another user's, and answers nothing for a user with none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L65)).\n- `InMemorySessionStore` omits a conversation that has aged out, as `latestFor` does ([validated by: omits a conversation that aged out and another user's, and answers nothing for a user with none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L65)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` filters on the user in the database, sorts on `{updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}` — the `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1}` index `latestFor` already needs serves its leading keys — and asks for a projection that leaves the entries behind ([validated by: asks the database to filter on the user, sort on the index with the id behind it, and never for the entries](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L108)).\n- It answers from that projection alone, so a collection that never returns an entry still lists conversations — the conversation does not leave the database to be listed ([validated by: answers from a projection alone, so a collection that never returns entries still lists conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L181)).\n\n## Paging\n\n- `limit` takes that many of the newest, and `before` resumes after a row, so a client pages by passing back the last summary it saw — `SessionCursor` is `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, which a `SessionSummary` already satisfies ([validated by: pages down the same order: a limit takes the newest, and before takes what is older than it](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L78)).\n- Paging walks every conversation exactly once even when all of them share an activity time, because the cursor carries the session id the order breaks ties on: a tie cannot fall between two pages and be skipped ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on activity rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L141)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` pages the same way ([validated by: pages with before and a limit, down the same newest-first order](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L62)).\n- It walks a tie exactly once too, through the same cursor ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on updatedAt rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L127)).\n- A list is at most 200 summaries however many are asked for, and 50 when none is asked for, so no caller can ask for an unbounded page ([validated by: answers at most two hundred conversations however many are asked for, and fifty when none is](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L94)).\n- That limit bounds the rows answered, not the rows the database reads to answer them: on `MongoSessionStore` the work is bounded by the `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}` index `docs/session-stores.md` prescribes, and under the two-key form Mongo sorts the user's whole history before applying the limit ([validated by: needs the three-key index to answer without sorting the whole history, which the two-key form does not](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L192)).\n- A fractional limit is floored rather than refused, since a slip in a limit is not a reason to deny a user their history ([validated by: floors a fractional limit, and reads a negative or unusable one as none given rather than as nothing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L104)).\n- A negative limit, and one that is not a usable number, are read as no limit given at all rather than as a limit of none: handing a user an empty history for a typo is the worse reading ([validated by: floors a fractional limit, and reads a negative or unusable one as none given rather than as nothing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L104)).\n- Only a limit of exactly `0` answers no conversations ([validated by: answers nothing only for a limit of exactly none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L116)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` answers a limit of `0` without querying, because MongoDB reads `limit: 0` as no limit at all and would otherwise answer with every conversation the filter matches ([validated by: answers a limit of none without asking the database, which reads a limit of zero as no limit at all](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L143)).\n\n## The two shipped stores answer one order\n\n- `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` list the same conversations in the same order, so a consumer swapping one for the other sees the same conversation list ([validated by: answer the same order for conversations whose activity differs](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L43)).\n- They break a tie on activity the same way, by session id ([validated by: break a tie on activity the same way, by session id](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L52)).\n- They page identically through the documented call, `listFor(userId, {limit, before: rows.at(-1)})`, a page at a time until a page comes back empty ([validated by: page the same way through the documented call, two rows at a time from the last row seen](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L61)).\n\n## A cursor the caller can actually hold\n\n- A cursor's `updatedAt` may be a `Date`, an ISO string or an epoch number, so a summary that went through JSON on its way to a client and back still pages ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, and refuses one carrying no usable moment](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L123)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` reads such a cursor as a date before it queries, since a string compares against no stored BSON date and would quietly match none of them ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, where a string would have matched no stored date](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L150)).\n- A cursor carrying no usable moment is refused with an error naming it, rather than read as no cursor at all, which would answer the first page again and repeat rows a client has already shown ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, and refuses one carrying no usable moment](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L123)).\n\n## A collection that cannot list\n\n- `find` is optional on `CollectionLike`, so an adapter written before conversation lists existed still satisfies the port ([validated by: refuses to list on a collection that implements no find, rather than reporting no conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L175)).\n- A store built on a collection without it refuses to list, rather than reporting that the user has no conversations ([validated by: refuses to list on a collection that implements no find, rather than reporting no conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L175)).\n\n## When the store cannot answer\n\n- A store whose collection fails rejects rather than answering a partial or empty list: what to show a user who may have conversations is the caller's decision, and an empty history is not a safe guess ([validated by: rejects rather than answering a partial list when the collection fails](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L225)).\n1:# WebSocket Protocol Specification\n3:| Field  | Value       |\n4:| ------ | ----------- |\n5:| Issue  | n/a         |\n6:| Status | In Progress |\n17:## 1. Introduction\n23:### 1.1 Terminology\n27:### 1.2 Transport\n31:## 2. Connection Establishment\n33:### 2.1 Endpoint\n50:### 2.2 Authentication\n71:- An upgrade whose `Host` header is missing or empty (RFC 6455 §4.1 requires one), or whose request-target is not a URL, such as `//`, is answered with HTTP 400 ([validated by: answers an empty Host, a missing Host and a // target with 400 instead of throwing out of the upgrade listener](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L68)).\n72:- The path check reads the target alone and never throws: a path beginning with `{basePath}/ws` passes, as §2.1 says; any other path, an empty target or `//` fails ([validated by: accepts any path beginning with /hal/ws and refuses another path, an empty target and //, without throwing](../../src/transport/ws/wsPath.test.ts#L4)).\n73:- A `WsAuthenticator` that throws synchronously, rather than returning a rejected promise, is answered with HTTP 500, as a rejection is ([validated by: answers 500 when the authenticator throws synchronously instead of rejecting](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L82)).\n74:- A `WsAuthenticator` that throws or rejects is logged at `error` as `authenticator failed` with its error's type alone, never its message, which can carry the credential it was checking ([validated by: logs a throwing or rejecting authenticator by its error type alone, never its message](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L92)).\n75:- A `WsAuthenticator` that returns a user directly, not in a promise, is treated like one that resolves to it, rather than failing inside the listener ([validated by: upgrades a request whose authenticator returns a user without a promise](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L118)).\n76:- A well-formed upgrade is unaffected ([validated by: still upgrades a well-formed request](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L126)).\n78:### 2.3 Session Initialization\n99:## 3. Message Format\n112:## 4. Client-to-Server Messages\n114:### 4.1 user_message\n125:| Field     | Type   | Required | Description                            |\n126:| --------- | ------ | -------- | -------------------------------------- |\n127:| `type`    | string | Yes      | `\"user_message\"`                       |\n128:| `content` | string | Yes      | Message text                           |\n130:Any other field is ignored, and the validator forwards only the fields the frame defines. In particular the server does not read a `chatId` from this message: the connection already determines the session ([validated by: drops any field the frame does not define, rather than forwarding it](../../src/transport/ws/validation.test.ts#L18)).\n140:- A session processes one `user_message` at a time, in the order the frames arrived: a frame arriving while an earlier one is still running waits, and its answer starts only after the earlier run's `stream_end` ([validated by: answers a second user_message only after the first has sent its stream_end, in the order they arrived](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L646)).\n141:- The waiting message's model request therefore holds the earlier answer finished, never one still streaming ([validated by: hands the second run a history whose first answer is finished, never one still streaming](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L677)).\n142:- A `ping` is answered at once, even while a run is pending ([validated by: answers a ping at once while a run is pending](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L695)).\n143:- A frame that fails validation and is not a `user_message`, such as a malformed `ping`, gets its `INVALID_MESSAGE` at once, even while a run is pending, but a `user_message` that fails validation is answered in turn, its `INVALID_MESSAGE` and `stream_end` after the run in progress, since an `error` frame names no message and would otherwise read as the running answer's ([validated by: answers an invalid ping at once but an invalid user_message in turn, after the run in progress](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L708)).\n144:- The reconnect flush (§ 11.3), which sends queued messages in one burst, is therefore safe: they are answered one after another, in the order the user typed them ([validated by: queues three messages sent in one tick before the handler returns, and answers them in that order](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L724)).\n145:- A queued message whose socket has closed by the time its turn comes is dropped, neither recorded nor answered, since nobody is left to read the answer and a session whose last socket closed has no credentials left ([validated by: drops a queued message whose socket closed before its turn, without asking the model or recording it](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L741)).\n147:### 4.2 ping\n158:| Field       | Type   | Required | Description                       |\n159:| ----------- | ------ | -------- | --------------------------------- |\n160:| `type`      | string | Yes      | `\"ping\"`                          |\n161:| `timestamp` | number | Yes      | Client timestamp in milliseconds  |\n163:## 5. Server-to-Client Messages\n165:### 5.1 connected\n169:| Field            | Type     | Description                                            |\n170:| ---------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |\n171:| `type`           | string   | `\"connected\"`                                          |\n172:| `sessionId`      | string   | UUID v4 identifying the session                        |\n173:| `message`        | string   | Human-readable greeting                                |\n174:| `examplePrompts` | string[] | Example queries derived from registered tool definitions |\n175:| `resumed`        | boolean  | Present only with resume enabled: whether the requested conversation was rejoined |\n176:| `entryCount`     | number   | Present only when `resumed` is `true`: how many stored entries the replay covers, skipped ones included |\n177:| `resumeFailure`  | string   | Present only when the connection named an id and `resumed` is `false`: `expired` when the caller's own session aged out, `unknown` otherwise. `unknown` deliberately covers both an id the server never issued and one that is not the caller's, so the answer reveals nothing about ids that exist |\n179:### 5.2 entry_upsert\n195:| Field   | Type         | Description                            |\n196:| ------- | ------------ | -------------------------------------- |\n197:| `type`  | string       | `\"entry_upsert\"`                       |\n198:| `index` | number       | Zero-based position in the entry array |\n199:| `entry` | SessionEntry | The entry object (see Section 6)       |\n208:### 5.3 entry_delta\n220:| Field   | Type   | Description                                        |\n221:| ------- | ------ | -------------------------------------------------- |\n222:| `type`  | string | `\"entry_delta\"`                                    |\n223:| `index` | number | Index of the entry to update                       |\n224:| `delta` | string | Text to concatenate to the entry's `content` field |\n232:### 5.4 entry_commit\n243:| Field   | Type   | Description                   |\n244:| ------- | ------ | ----------------------------- |\n245:| `type`  | string | `\"entry_commit\"`              |\n246:| `index` | number | Index of the entry to commit  |\n253:### 5.5 entry_skip\n255:Notifies the client that a session entry was created on the server and will never be sent. This happens under output suppression (see [tool-responses.md](../hal-engine-tool-responses/spec.md)): the server keeps the entry so the model retains context, and the client does not need it for display ([validated by: still records a suppressed reply in the session, so history keeps it](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L211)).\n264:| Field   | Type   | Description                                    |\n265:| ------- | ------ | ---------------------------------------------- |\n266:| `type`  | string | `\"entry_skip\"`                                 |\n267:| `index` | number | Server-side index of the skipped entry         |\n271:- An entry **opened while suppression is already active** is announced with `entry_skip` and nothing else: no `entry_upsert`, no `entry_delta`, no `entry_commit` follows it ([validated by: skips rather than upserts a segment opened after suppression](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L203)).\n272:- An entry **already sent before suppression began** cannot be skipped, because the client is displaying it. The server retracts it instead, by re-sending `entry_upsert` at the same index with the entry's `content` set to `\"\"`. The client renders an empty assistant entry as nothing, so the text disappears ([validated by: blanks an assistant entry that was already sent](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L180)).\n273:- An entry **still streaming when suppression begins** is first committed as streamed so far, with `entry_commit`, and is from then on an entry already sent: an answer is retracted as above, a thought stays as the client saw it, and whatever the model writes next opens a new entry announced with `entry_skip` ([validated by: commits and retracts an answer still open when suppression begins, and skips what follows as a new entry](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L267), [validated by: commits a thought still open when suppression begins as the client saw it, and skips what follows](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L288)).\n275:#### Limits of retraction\n277:- Only assistant entries are ever retracted; a thinking entry is never blanked, even when it was sent before suppression began ([validated by: never blanks a thinking entry, only assistant ones](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L195)).\n278:- Each sent entry is blanked once, so a second suppression in the same stream repeats nothing ([validated by: blanks each sent entry once, so a second suppression repeats nothing](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L229)).\n279:- An entry the client only ever saw as `entry_skip` is never retracted, because nothing is displayed to retract ([validated by: does not resurrect an entry the client only ever saw skipped](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L221)).\n289:### 5.6 error\n301:| Field     | Type   | Description              |","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c9f653e3-de50-40d7-9dab-0757a826ee31","uuid":"cfc22170-de03-4419-a275-e3a01cfcdc1a","timestamp":"2026-11-06T20:44:51.350Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# Conversation List\n\n| Field  | Value                 |\n| ------ | --------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#143 |\n| Status | Shipped               |\n\nResume gets a client back into one conversation: the id it names, or the user's most recently active one. Neither tells it which conversations exist, and every other member of `SessionStore` answers about a session the caller can already name, so a consumer wanting to offer a conversation list had to query the collection behind the engine's back, against a document shape and a key the engine owns. `listFor` answers the list instead, as summaries rather than sessions. **GDPR.** A summary is personal data — that a user held a conversation, and when — so it carries no conversation content, it is filtered to one user by the query rather than in application code afterwards, and the retention bound stays the consumer's, through the store's `maxAgeMs` and erasure methods and re-cinq/HALEngine#41.\n\n## The summary\n\n- A summary is `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount`, and nothing else: neither the conversation's entries nor its credentials, so a list can never hand over either, even for a session created with credentials ([validated by: carries only its four fields, so a list can never hand over a conversation or a credential](../../src/infrastructure/stores/sessionStoreListSignature.test.ts#L22)).\n- `listFor` is optional, like `latestFor` and `lookup` before it: a store written against the five original members still satisfies the interface, and both shipped stores implement it ([validated by: leaves a store with only the five original members satisfying the interface beside both shipped ones](../../src/infrastructure/stores/sessionStoreListSignature.test.ts#L11)).\n\n## The order a list answers in\n\n- Summaries come back most recent activity first ([validated by: answers newest activity first, headed by the very session latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L27)).\n- The first summary is the session `latestFor` returns, because both read the same activity ([validated by: answers newest activity first, headed by the very session latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L27)).\n- The order is total rather than merely sorted: two conversations last active in the same millisecond are ordered by session id, the larger first, which is what lets a page resume without dropping one of them ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on activity rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L141)).\n- In `InMemorySessionStore` that tie-break is the list's alone: `latestFor` breaks the same tie by creation order, so for two tied conversations the head of the list and the session a resume rejoins can differ ([validated by: breaks a tie on activity by session id, where latestFor takes the session created later](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L42)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` answers in order of `updatedAt`, which each `save` moves forward, and its first summary is the session its own `latestFor` returns ([validated by: answers the user's conversations newest saved first, headed by the one latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L31)).\n- It breaks a tie by `_id` in both members, so there the head of the list is the session a resume rejoins even when every conversation shares an `updatedAt` ([validated by: heads the list with the session latestFor returns even when every conversation shares an updatedAt](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L161)).\n\n## What a summary reports\n\n- `InMemorySessionStore` reports the moment `create` was called, the newest entry time as the activity, and the conversation's entry count ([validated by: reports a creation time, the newest activity and the entry count of each conversation](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L53)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` reports the document's `createdAt`, the `updatedAt` its last save wrote, and the counter written beside the entries ([validated by: reports the creation time, the last save and the entry count of each conversation](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L49)).\n- That counter is what a save writes, so a list never reads a conversation to count one ([validated by: counts a conversation from the field a save writes beside the entries](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L95)).\n- A document written before the counter existed reads as a conversation of no entries rather than failing the list, which is what the backlog documented in `docs/session-stores.md` is for ([validated by: reads a conversation saved before the counter existed as one of no entries, rather than failing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L79)).\n\n## Whose conversations\n\n- A list holds only the asking user's conversations: another user's never appear, and a user with none gets an empty list ([validated by: omits a conversation that aged out and another user's, and answers nothing for a user with none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L65)).\n- `InMemorySessionStore` omits a conversation that has aged out, as `latestFor` does ([validated by: omits a conversation that aged out and another user's, and answers nothing for a user with none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L65)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` filters on the user in the database, sorts on `{updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}` — the `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1}` index `latestFor` already needs serves its leading keys — and asks for a projection that leaves the entries behind ([validated by: asks the database to filter on the user, sort on the index with the id behind it, and never for the entries](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L108)).\n- It answers from that projection alone, so a collection that never returns an entry still lists conversations — the conversation does not leave the database to be listed ([validated by: answers from a projection alone, so a collection that never returns entries still lists conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L181)).\n\n## Paging\n\n- `limit` takes that many of the newest, and `before` resumes after a row, so a client pages by passing back the last summary it saw — `SessionCursor` is `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, which a `SessionSummary` already satisfies ([validated by: pages down the same order: a limit takes the newest, and before takes what is older than it](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L78)).\n- Paging walks every conversation exactly once even when all of them share an activity time, because the cursor carries the session id the order breaks ties on: a tie cannot fall between two pages and be skipped ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on activity rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L141)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` pages the same way ([validated by: pages with before and a limit, down the same newest-first order](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L62)).\n- It walks a tie exactly once too, through the same cursor ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on updatedAt rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L127)).\n- A list is at most 200 summaries however many are asked for, and 50 when none is asked for, so no caller can ask for an unbounded page ([validated by: answers at most two hundred conversations however many are asked for, and fifty when none is](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L94)).\n- That limit bounds the rows answered, not the rows the database reads to answer them: on `MongoSessionStore` the work is bounded by the `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}` index `docs/session-stores.md` prescribes, and under the two-key form Mongo sorts the user's whole history before applying the limit ([validated by: needs the three-key index to answer without sorting the whole history, which the two-key form does not](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L192)).\n- A fractional limit is floored rather than refused, since a slip in a limit is not a reason to deny a user their history ([validated by: floors a fractional limit, and reads a negative or unusable one as none given rather than as nothing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L104)).\n- A negative limit, and one that is not a usable number, are read as no limit given at all rather than as a limit of none: handing a user an empty history for a typo is the worse reading ([validated by: floors a fractional limit, and reads a negative or unusable one as none given rather than as nothing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L104)).\n- Only a limit of exactly `0` answers no conversations ([validated by: answers nothing only for a limit of exactly none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L116)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` answers a limit of `0` without querying, because MongoDB reads `limit: 0` as no limit at all and would otherwise answer with every conversation the filter matches ([validated by: answers a limit of none without asking the database, which reads a limit of zero as no limit at all](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L143)).\n\n## The two shipped stores answer one order\n\n- `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` list the same conversations in the same order, so a consumer swapping one for the other sees the same conversation list ([validated by: answer the same order for conversations whose activity differs](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L43)).\n- They break a tie on activity the same way, by session id ([validated by: break a tie on activity the same way, by session id](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L52)).\n- They page identically through the documented call, `listFor(userId, {limit, before: rows.at(-1)})`, a page at a time until a page comes back empty ([validated by: page the same way through the documented call, two rows at a time from the last row seen](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L61)).\n\n## A cursor the caller can actually hold\n\n- A cursor's `updatedAt` may be a `Date`, an ISO string or an epoch number, so a summary that went through JSON on its way to a client and back still pages ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, and refuses one carrying no usable moment](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L123)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` reads such a cursor as a date before it queries, since a string compares against no stored BSON date and would quietly match none of them ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, where a string would have matched no stored date](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L150)).\n- A cursor carrying no usable moment is refused with an error naming it, rather than read as no cursor at all, which would answer the first page again and repeat rows a client has already shown ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, and refuses one carrying no usable moment](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L123)).\n\n## A collection that cannot list\n\n- `find` is optional on `CollectionLike`, so an adapter written before conversation lists existed still satisfies the port ([validated by: refuses to list on a collection that implements no find, rather than reporting no conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L175)).\n- A store built on a collection without it refuses to list, rather than reporting that the user has no conversations ([validated by: refuses to list on a collection that implements no find, rather than reporting no conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L175)).\n\n## When the store cannot answer\n\n- A store whose collection fails rejects rather than answering a another, in the order the user typed them ([validated by: queues three messages sent in one tick before the handler returns, and answers them in that order](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L724)).\n145:- A queued message whose socket has closed by the time its turn comes is dropped, neither recorded nor answered, since nobody is left to read the answer and a session whose last socket closed has no credentials left ([validated by: drops a queued message whose socket closed before its turn, without asking the model or recording it](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L741)).\n147:### 4.2 ping\n158:| Field       | Type   | Required | Description                       |\n159:| ----------- | ------ | -------- | --------------------------------- |\n160:| `type`      | string | Yes      | `\"ping\"`                          |\n161:| `timestamp` | number | Yes      | Client timestamp in milliseconds  |\n163:## 5. Server-to-Client Messages\n165:### 5.1 connected\n169:| Field            | Type     | Description                                            |\n170:| ---------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |\n171:| `type`           | string   | `\"connected\"`                                          |\n172:| `sessionId`      | string   | UUID v4 identifying the session                        |\n173:| `message`        | string   | Human-readable greeting                                |\n174:| `examplePrompts` | string[] | Example queries derived from registered tool definitions |\n175:| `resumed`        | boolean  | Present only with resume enabled: whether the requested conversation was rejoined |\n176:| `entryCount`     | number   | Present only when `resumed` is `true`: how many stored entries the replay covers, skipped ones included |\n177:| `resumeFailure`  | string   | Present only when the connection named an id and `resumed` is `false`: `expired` when the caller's own session aged out, `unknown` otherwise. `unknown` deliberately covers both an id the server never issued and one that is not the caller's, so the answer reveals nothing about ids that exist |\n179:### 5.2 entry_upsert\n195:| Field   | Type         | Description                            |\n196:| ------- | ------------ | -------------------------------------- |\n197:| `type`  | string       | `\"entry_upsert\"`                       |\n198:| `index` | number       | Zero-based position in the entry array |\n199:| `entry` | SessionEntry | The entry object (see Section 6)       |\n208:### 5.3 entry_delta\n220:| Field   | Type   | Description                                        |\n221:| ------- | ------ | -------------------------------------------------- |\n222:| `type`  | string | `\"entry_delta\"`                                    |\n223:| `index` | number | Index of the entry to update                       |\n224:| `delta` | string | Text to concatenate to the entry's `content` field |\n232:### 5.4 entry_commit\n243:| Field   | Type   | Description                   |\n244:| ------- | ------ | ----------------------------- |\n245:| `type`  | string | `\"entry_commit\"`              |\n246:| `index` | number | Index of the entry to commit  |\n253:### 5.5 entry_skip\n255:Notifies the client that a session entry was created on the server and will never be sent. This happens under output suppression (see [tool-responses.md](../hal-engine-tool-responses/spec.md)): the server keeps the entry so the model retains context, and the client does not need it for display ([validated by: still records a suppressed reply in the session, so history keeps it](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L211)).\n264:| Field   | Type   | Description                                    |\n265:| ------- | ------ | ---------------------------------------------- |\n266:| `type`  | string | `\"entry_skip\"`                                 |\n267:| `index` | number | Server-side index of the skipped entry         |\n271:- An entry **opened while suppression is already active** is announced with `entry_skip` and nothing else: no `entry_upsert`, no `entry_delta`, no `entry_commit` follows it ([validated by: skips rather than upserts a segment opened after suppression](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L203)).\n272:- An entry **already sent before suppression began** cannot be skipped, because the client is displaying it. The server retracts it instead, by re-sending `entry_upsert` at the same index with the entry's `content` set to `\"\"`. The client renders an empty assistant entry as nothing, so the text disappears ([validated by: blanks an assistant entry that was already sent](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L180)).\n273:- An entry **still streaming when suppression begins** is first committed as streamed so far, with `entry_commit`, and is from then on an entry already sent: an answer is retracted as above, a thought stays as the client saw it, and whatever the model writes next opens a new entry announced with `entry_skip` ([validated by: commits and retracts an answer still open when suppression begins, and skips what follows as a new entry](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L267), [validated by: commits a thought still open when suppression begins as the client saw it, and skips what follows](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L288)).\n275:#### Limits of retraction\n277:- Only assistant entries are ever retracted; a thinking entry is never blanked, even when it was sent before suppression began ([validated by: never blanks a thinking entry, only assistant ones](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L195)).\n278:- Each sent entry is blanked once, so a second suppression in the same stream repeats nothing ([validated by: blanks each sent entry once, so a second suppression repeats nothing](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L229)).\n279:- An entry the client only ever saw as `entry_skip` is never retracted, because nothing is displayed to retract ([validated by: does not resurrect an entry the client only ever saw skipped](../../src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts#L221)).\n289:### 5.6 error\n301:| Field     | Type   | Description              |","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01NGcwTA8xhXknix67qKb4UH","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
